0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!\infty}
, Property
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.1 encodes the fact that only two localized extremal high-probability sets
A_{0}^{\varepsilon}
and
A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
are necessary to assess the regularity of the score in (
C
) and (
D
).
Here, the threshold
C_{\star}^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{-C_{\star}}
corresponds to the time after which
s^{\star}(t,x)
is essentially as smooth as
s^{\star}(\infty,x)
.

2.

Property
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.2 captures the fact that for fixed
t
, the high-probability sets
A_{t}^{\varepsilon}
expand as
\varepsilon
decreases.
This typically will be fulfilled if
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!0}
and
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!\infty}
do not decay too fast at the boundary of their supports.
Such a quantitative lower bound on the distance between
A_{t}^{\varepsilon}
and the boundary of
A_{t}^{\varepsilon^{2}}
ensures minimal growth of
(A_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
. This is key for constructing globally one-sided Lipschitz neural networks (see Proposition
16
) and for probabilistic control of the trajectories
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle X\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle X\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle X\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle X\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}
staying within regularity zones of
s^{\star}(t,\cdot)
, crucial for deriving generalization bounds (see Proposition
12
).

For these reasons, modulo a time reparametrization of the forward process, we expect that Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
accurately capture the behavior of score functions for a broad class of generative diffusions initialized from
\beta
-smooth distributions. We therefore take them as interesting in their own right.

3
A generic two-step analysis of generative diffusions

In this section, we will outline the key steps leading to guarantees for SGMs.
We intentionally avoid committing to a specific statistical model to maintain generality.
The analysis involves two complimentary parts: the stability of the backward equations (Section
3.1
), and the generalization accuracy of score matching for score estimation (Section
3.2
).

3.1
Quantitative stability of (stochastic) differential equations

Recalling the methodology of SGMs (Section
1.1.2
), we notice that the (stochastic) differential equations governing dynamics of the true backward (
36
) and the approximate backward (
37
) differ only in their initial distribution and their drifts, but share the same diffusion coefficient.
When measuring discrepancy in Wasserstein distance
\mathrm{W}_{1}
, this leads us to examine the marginal stability of stochastic differential equation solutions under variations in the initial distribution and drift term.
Unlike
Oko et al. 2023
;
Fukumizu et al. 2024
;
Chen et al. 2023b
;
Gentiloni-Silveri and Ocello 2025
, which operate within an
L^{2}
framework adapted to
\mathrm{W}_{2}
and Kullback-Leibler, our results yield
L^{1}
-type stability, which arises more naturally when dealing with
\mathrm{W}_{1}
distance.

In the first place, we bound the error arising from the mismatch between initial distributions
p_{0}=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!\overline{T}}
for the true backward and
\widehat{p}_{0}=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!\infty}
for the approximate backward.

Lemma 10

(Stability with respect to the initial condition)
.

Let
\underline{\tau}\leq\overline{\tau}
, and
p_{\underline{\tau}},\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}
be two
L
-subGaussian probability densities on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
a:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a vector field such that for all
t
,
\|a_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq V
.
Let
r:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a space-homogeneous diffusion coefficient such that
\|r\|_{L^{2}([\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}])}\leq M
.
Consider processes
(X_{t})_{\underline{\tau}\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}}
and
(\widetilde{X}_{t})_{\underline{\tau}\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}}
defined on
[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]
, solutions to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}

\displaystyle=a_{t}(X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad X_{\underline{\tau}}\sim p_{\underline{\tau}},

\displaystyle d\widetilde{X}_{t}

\displaystyle=a_{t}(\widetilde{X}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad\widetilde{X}_{\underline{\tau}}\sim\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}.

Write
X_{t}\sim p_{t}
and
\widetilde{X}_{t}\sim\widetilde{p}_{t}
.
Then for all
0<\varepsilon<1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{\tau}},\widetilde{p}_{\overline{\tau}})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl((\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})V+\sqrt{d}M+\sqrt{L\log(\varepsilon^{-1})}\bigr)\|p_{\underline{\tau}}-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+L\varepsilon/2.

The proof of Lemma
10
can be found in Section
A.4
.
In contrast to other stability bounds that require smoothness of the drift terms
Backhoff-Veraguas et al. 2022
, the assumptions on
a
and
r
here are particularly mild, amounting merely to boundedness of order zero.
On the other hand, the result relies on a subGaussian assumption for the initial distributions, which enables a quantitative link between the information-theoretic discrepancy
\|p_{\underline{\tau}}-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}\|_{L^{1}}
and the geometry-aware Wasserstein distance
\mathrm{W}_{1}
.

In the second place, we bound the drift error
a-\widehat{a}=\bigl(\sigma^{2}+b^{2}\bigr)(s^{\star}-\widehat{s})
arising from the mismatch between the true score
s^{\star}
and its estimated counterpart
\widehat{s}
in (
36
) and (
37
) respectively.
We first deal with the most general case with no constraint on the diffusion coefficient.

Theorem 11

(Stability with respect to the drift — general case)
.

Let
\underline{\tau}\leq\overline{\tau}
, and
p_{\underline{\tau}}
be a probability density on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
a,\bar{a}:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be vector fields. Assume that for all
t
,
\bar{a}_{t}
is locally Lipschitz and
\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{a}_{t})\leq\bar{L}_{t}<\infty
.
Let
r:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a space-homogeneous diffusion coefficient.
Consider processes
(X_{t})_{\underline{\tau}\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}}
and
(\bar{X}_{t})_{\underline{\tau}\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}}
defined on
[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]
, solutions to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}

\displaystyle=a_{t}(X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad X_{\underline{\tau}}\sim p_{\underline{\tau}},

\displaystyle d\bar{X}_{t}

\displaystyle=\bar{a}_{t}(\bar{X}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}\sim p_{\underline{\tau}}.

Write
X_{t}\sim p_{t}
and
\bar{X}_{t}\sim\bar{p}_{t}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{\tau}},\bar{p}_{\overline{\tau}})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\int_{t}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{L}_{u}\mathrm{d}u}\|a_{t}(x)-\bar{a}_{t}(x)\|p_{t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The proof of Theorem
11
can be found in Section
A.5
.
Note the asymmetry between
a,p
and
\bar{a},\bar{p}
in the assumptions and statement of the result.
Namely, the bound involves error integration against
p_{t}
(interpreted as the true marginal), with an additional exponential weight depending on the largest eigenvalue of the Jacobian of
\bar{a}_{t}
(interpreted as an approximate drift).
Although this integrated exponential weighting term might seem harmful, recall that Proposition
7
ensures that the true score function
s^{\star}
satisfies

\int_{0}^{\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\max}(\nabla s^{\star}(u,x)+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d\times d})\mathrm{d}u\leq C<\infty.

Therefore, one may reasonably expect to construct an approximation of
s^{\star}
that preserves this quantitative one-sided Lipschitz property without introducing additional statistical limitations.

3.2
Quantitative oracle bounds for score matching

To complement the analytical stability results of the previous section, we now turn to the statistical generalization properties of the denoising score matching estimator (
39
). As is common in non-asymptotic analyses of empirical risk minimization, the result takes the form of a general oracle inequality in expectation.

With transparent notation, we denote by
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{S}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]}}
the set of time-restrictions
s:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
of functions
s\in\mathcal{S}
possibly defined over a larger domain.
The quantity
\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{S},\|\cdot\|,\delta)
denotes the covering number at scale
\delta>0
of the set
\mathfrak{S}
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|
.

Proposition 12

(Oracle inequality for denoising score matching)
.

Assume that
p^{\star}
is
K
-subGaussian and that
s^{\star}
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.
Let
X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)}
be a i.i.d. sample from
p^{\star}(x)\mathrm{d}x
, and assume that
\sigma>0
.
Let
\widehat{s}
be an empirical risk minimizer of the denoising score matching loss (
39
)

\displaystyle\widehat{s}\in\argmin_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]}(s,X^{(i)}),

for
n^{-1}\leq\underline{\tau}<\overline{\tau}
, over a class
\mathcal{S}
of functions
s:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\sup_{t\in[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]}\|s(t,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq V
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|s(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\frac{c_{n}}{n}\log\Big(\mathcal{N}\big({\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{S}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},n^{-1}\bigr)\vee n\Bigr),

where
c_{n}:=C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)
and
g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V):=\left(1+V^{2}+(1+\sigma+\sigma^{-1})^{4}(1+\underline{\tau}^{-1})+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}(1-e^{-2\underline{\tau}})}\right)
.

The proof of Proposition
12
can be found in Section
A.6
, where is also established a high probability deviation bound.
This result is similar to
(
Oko et al. 2023
, Theorem 4.3)
,
while circumventing the critical issue pointed out
by
Yakovlev and Puchkin 2025
.
Its proof builds upon standard Bernstein-type concentration arguments.
Since the contrast function
\gamma(s,x)
is unbounded, we use a clipping strategy.
The subGaussianity of the underlying distribution
p^{\star}
enables us to control the effect of this clipping, here limited to
\mathrm{polylog}(n)
factors only.

Recall that the Proposition
12
applies to an Orstein-Uhlenbeck forward process (
1
).
Indeed, this forward process allows for the denoising score trick (Theorem
3
), which grounds the empirical risk minimization strategy.
Hence, this result can then be adapted to other choices of forward processes, provided that a similar score trick holds.

4
From score regularity to guarantees for score matching

With all the analytical and statistical groundwork now in place, this section establishes that the subGaussianity of
p^{\star}
combined with the smoothness Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
of the score function suffice for denoising score matching to be provably successful over a suitably chosen class of neural networks.
We defer the precise construction of the neural networks to Section
4.1
.

The following lemma shows that if the score satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
, then score-based generative models can achieve minimax convergence rates up to logarithmic factors.

Lemma 13

(Score smoothness implies estimation rates for score matching)
.

Let
X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)}
be a i.i.d. sample with density
p^{\star}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Write
\widehat{p}
for the distribution of
\widehat{X}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}
, obtained from the backward dynamic (
37
) with
\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\overline{T}-\underline{T}])}\leq C
,
C^{-1}\leq\sigma\leq C
, and with a score estimated via denoising score matching (
39
) over class
\mathcal{S}
on time interval
[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
.

Assume that
p^{\star}(x)\mathrm{d}x
is
K
-subGaussian (
40
), and that the score function
s^{\star}
(
35
) associated to
p^{\star}
satisfies
Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
with
\beta>0
.
Then there exists

(C_{2}n)^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}\leq\underline{T}\leq\overline{T}\leq C_{2}\log n
, and a class of neural networks
\mathcal{S}
with
\tanh
activation function (see Section
4.1
), such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{W}_{1}(p^{\star},\widehat{p})]\leq C_{3}\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

We showcase the proof of Lemma
13
in Section
4.3
to highlight its simplicity.
This result illustrates how score regularity can yield minimax convergence rates of SGMs, no matter the value of the backward diffusion coefficient
(b_{t})_{t}
.
In short, the one-sided Lipschitz regularity (
B
) allows to bound the Wasserstein distance by a
boundedly
-weighted
L^{1}
distance between the true score and its estimator (Theorem
11
).
On the other hand, the high order smoothness of the score (
C
) and (
D
) allows to use classical neural networks approximation theory to bound this latter distance through a bias-variance trade-off (Proposition
12
).

Let us recall that from Lemma
9
, the score
s^{\star}(t,x)
of a distribution
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
. Lemma
13
hence directly directly yields quantitative generalization bounds over
\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
(see Theorem
I
).

4.1
Time-dependent neural network structure

To prove Lemma
13
, let us first introduce the class
\mathcal{S}
of neural networks used for constructing an effective score estimator.
The class of neural networks is designed to be spatially one-sided Lipschitz to ensure the finiteness of the exponential term appearing in Theorem
11
.
It relies on the following building block.

Definition 14

(Time-dependent neural network class)
.

We denote by
\Psi(L,W,B,V,V^{{}^{\prime}})_{d}^{k+1}
the class of
\tanh
neural networks
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{k+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
with:

•

at most
L
hidden layers, each of maximum width
W
, and having weights bounded by
B
.

•

\|\phi\|_{\infty}\leq V
.

•

\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\phi(t,x))\leq V^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Time discretization.

The construction builds upon a partition of the time interval
[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
into
m
coarse sub-intervals
[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]
, which are themselves further subdivided into
\Upsilon_{k}
finer sub-intervals
[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
, yielding

\displaystyle[\underline{T},\overline{T}]=\bigcup_{k=0}^{m-1}[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}],\text{\penalty\ \penalty\ and\penalty\ \penalty\ }[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]=\bigcup_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}],

with
\tau_{k}=\tau_{k,0}
and
\tau_{k,\Upsilon_{k}}=\tau_{k+1}
.
For each finer interval
[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
, we employ a separate neural network of the form of Definition
14
(see (
43
) below).

We set
\underline{T}:=(Cn)^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}
,
\overline{T}:=C\log n
, and
m:=\log_{2}(\overline{T}/\underline{T})\in\mathbb{N}
without loss of generality. Note by now that
m\leq C\log n
.
We consider the subdivision
(\tau_{k})_{0\leq k\leq m}
of
[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
with extremal points
\underline{T}=:\tau_{0}<\tau_{m}:=\overline{T}
, and geometric progression
\tau_{k+1}/\tau_{k}=2
, meaning that
\tau_{k}:=2^{k}\underline{T}=2^{k-m}\overline{T}
.

For all
k\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}
, we consider the subdivision
(\tau_{k,j})_{0\leq j\leq\Upsilon_{k}}
of
[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]
with extremal points
\tau_{k}=:\tau_{k,0}<\tau_{k,\Upsilon_{k}}:=\tau_{k+1}
, and arithmetic progression
\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j}=\tau_{k}/\Upsilon_{k}
, meaning that

\tau_{k,j}:=\left(1+j/\Upsilon_{k}\right)\tau_{k}\text{,\penalty\ \penalty\ where\penalty\ \penalty\ }\Upsilon_{k}=\lceil(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{\frac{d-2}{d}}\rceil.

(42)

Global architecture.

Using notation of Definition
14
, the chosen class of candidate scores is

\mathcal{S}:=\left\{-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d\times d}+\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}S_{k,j}(t,\cdot)\mathds{1}_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1})}\Big|\ S_{k,j}\in\Psi(L_{k},\mathrm{W}_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d}\right\},

(43)

with time-dependent fully connected architectures of sizes

L_{k}=C,\quad W_{k}=C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{\frac{d-2}{2\beta+d}},\quad B_{k}=Cn^{C_{2}},

(44)

and functional constraints

V_{k}=C\log(n)^{C_{2}}(\tau_{k}\wedge 1)^{-1/2},\quad V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}=Ce^{-\tau_{k}}(\tau_{k}\wedge 1)^{-1+\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{d}}.

(45)

The sup-norm scaling
V_{k}
is chosen to cover that of the true score over
[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]
(see Theorem
8
), with the one-sided Lipschitz scaling
V_{k}^{\prime}
handled similarly (see Proposition
7
).
We incorporate the term
-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d\times d}
into the network class to incorporate the known limit of the score as
t\to\infty
.
This design allows the network to naturally follow the exponential decay of the true score, leading to the guarantee

\lambda_{\max}(\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d\times d}+\nabla\widehat{s}(t,x))\lesssim e^{-t}
when
t
is large.

4.2
Leveraging score regularity for neural networks approximation

The smoothness of the score function enables efficient approximation by neural networks. In particular, we make use of the following classical result from neural network approximation theory.

Proposition 15

(Fixed-time approximation of
\gamma
-Hölder functions —
(
De Ryck et al. 2021
, Theorem 5.1)
)
.

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
\gamma,A,R>0
, there exists
C_{\gamma},C_{\gamma,2}>0
such that the neural networks class

\mathbb{K}:=\Psi\Big(2,C_{\gamma}(A\varepsilon^{-1})^{d},C_{\gamma}R\varepsilon^{-C_{\gamma}},C_{\gamma}R,\infty\Big)_{d}^{d}

satisfies for all
\theta\in(0,1)

\log\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{K},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\theta)\bigr)\leq C_{\gamma,2}(A\varepsilon^{-1})^{d}\log\bigl(R(\varepsilon\theta)^{-1}\bigr),

and for all
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{R}(\mathrm{B}(0,A),\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists a neural network
N_{f}\in\mathbb{K}
satisfying for all
k\in\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\gamma\rfloor\}

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathrm{B}(0,A)}\|\nabla^{k}f(x)-\nabla^{k}N_{f}(x)\|\leq CR\varepsilon^{\gamma-k}.

From this result applied to fixed-time scores functions
s^{\star}(\tau_{k},\cdot)
, and local Taylor-like neural network constructions in the time variable, we deduce the following approximation result.

Proposition 16

(Time-dependent score estimation)
.

Let
p^{\star}
a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that its score function
s^{\star}
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.
Then for all
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,m-1\}
and
j\in\{0,\ldots,\Upsilon_{k}-1\}
, there exists
\bar{s}\in\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d}
such that

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}}\|s^{\star}(t,x)-\bigl(\bar{s}(t,x)-\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}x\bigr)\|\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}},

where
\tau_{k,j}
defined in (
42
)
and
\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d}
defined in Definition
14
. Furthermore, we have

\log\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},n^{-1})\bigr)\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}}{\tau_{k}}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}.

The proof of Proposition
16
can be found in Section
A.7
. This result illustrates how the adaptive regularity of the score function translates directly into efficient neural network approximations. In particular, it ensures that one can construct neural networks that are both accurate and one-sided Lipschitz, thereby controlling the exponential stability term appearing in Theorem
11
.

4.3
Score matching rates from score regularity

Having defined the neural network class
\mathcal{S}
for the score estimator in (
43
), we can now proceed to the proof of Lemma
13
.
The proof follows three main steps:

1.

Using the stability results of Section
3
, we show that the the problem reduces to controlling the
L^{1}
-distance between the true score and its estimator.

2.

Applying the oracle bound of Section
3.2
, we then bound this
L^{1}
-distance in terms of a trade-off between the best approximation error achievable by the class
\mathcal{S}
and its complexity on each sub-interval
[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
.

3.

Finally, using the universal approximation theory of neural networks from Section
4.2
, we show that this trade-off leads to the desired minimax convergence rate.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

As pointed out in Section
1.1.2
, the distribution
\widehat{p}:=\widehat{p}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}\sim\widehat{X}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}
accumulates three distinct errors

•

Initial condition.

The true backward process is initialized at
p_{0}=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern , while the approximated backward is initiated at
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern .

•

Score error.

The true backward process uses the true score
s^{\star}(t,\cdot)
, while the approximated backward process uses the estimator
\widehat{s}(t,\cdot)
.

•

Early stopping.

The true backward process should be run for time
\overline{T}
to recover distribution
p_{\overline{T}}=\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!0}=p^{\star}
, while the approximate backward is stopped earlier, at time
\overline{T}-\underline{T}
.

Early stopping.

Let us first bound the error arising from the early stopping.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widehat{p},p^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widehat{p},\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern Initial condition.

We first bound the error arising from the initial condition.
To this aim, let
\widetilde{\mu}_{t}
denote the distribution of the solution

to the equation

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{X}_{t}=-\widehat{a}_{t}(\widetilde{X}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}b_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad\widetilde{X}_{0}\sim\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern Score error.

Let us now bound the second term of (
62
) which corresponds to the score approximation error. As the score estimation is done independently on each intervals
[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
, we first decompose the distance

\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}})
into contributions that involve only the dynamics within each time sub-interval
[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
.
More precisely, let
p_{t}^{k,j}
denote the distribution of the solution
X^{k,j}_{t}
to the equation

\mathrm{d}X^{k,j}_{t}=-\widehat{a}_{t}^{k,j}(X^{k,j}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}b_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad X^{k,j}_{0}\sim\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!\overline{T}},

over
[0,\overline{T}-\underline{T}]
,
with

\displaystyle\widehat{a}_{t}^{k,j}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}a_{t}&\text{if }t\leq\overline{T}-\tau_{k,j},\\
\widehat{a}_{t}&\text{if }t>\overline{T}-\tau_{k,j}.\end{array}\right.

From triangle inequality again,

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}})\leq\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}^{k,j+1},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}^{k,j}).

Now, using the stability of SDEs with respect to the drift (Theorem
11
) on each pair of stochastic processes
\bigl((X^{k,j+1}_{t})_{t},(X^{k,j}_{t})_{t}\bigr)
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\int_{\overline{T}-\tau_{k,j+1}}^{\overline{T}-\tau_{k,j}}e^{\int_{t}^{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}\|\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\widehat{a}_{u}))\|_{\infty}\mathrm{d}u}\int\|\widehat{s}(\overline{T}-t,x)-s^{\star}(\overline{T}-t,x)\|p_{t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\int_{\tau_{k,j}}^{\tau_{k,j+1}}e^{\int_{\overline{T}-t}^{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}\|\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\widehat{a}_{u}))\|_{\infty}\mathrm{d}u}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

As the class of estimators
\mathcal{S}
is composed of neural networks
\phi
satisfying

\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}I_{d\times d}+\nabla\phi(t,\cdot)\right)\leq Ce^{-t}(1+t^{-1+\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{d}}),

we deduce that

\int_{0}^{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}\|\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\widehat{a}_{u})\|_{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\leq C,

and hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}})

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\int_{\tau_{k,j}}^{\tau_{k,j+1}}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Then, applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}},p_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}})

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\sqrt{\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j}}\left(\int_{\tau_{k,j}}^{\tau_{k,j+1}}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern We now use the bias-variance bound from Proposition
12
and the neural network approximation of Proposition

to control the score approximation. Recall that in Proposition

, the approximation properties hold uniformly over

and

, where

.

For all

and

that
\|s(t,x)\|\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}(1+\tau_{k}^{-1/2})
and from (
A
) we have
\|s^{\star}(t,x)\|\leq C(1+t^{-1})(1+\|x\|)
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{(A_{\tau_{k}})^{c}}\|s(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern \displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}(\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j})\left(\inf_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\|s-s^{\star}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}([\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]\times A_{\tau_{k}})}+(1+\tau_{k}^{-2})n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{n}\log(n)^{C_{2}}(\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j})\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}\log\mathcal{N}\bigl({\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{S}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},n^{-1}\bigr).

(119)

Let us apply Proposition
16
to obtain the bound on the covering number and approximation error of the class
\mathcal{S}
.
We first treat the case

. In this case

, so that we have to bound

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\{X^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\Bigg[\tau_{k}\int_{\tau_{k}}^{\tau_{k+1}}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern since
\tau_{k+1}=2\tau_{k}
.
Combining (
4.3
) and the approximation results of Proposition
16
leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{X^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\Bigg[\tau_{k}\int_{\tau_{k}}^{\tau_{k+1}}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}\left(\tau_{k}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{k}}{\tau_{k}}\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}n^{\frac{d-2}{2\beta+d}}\right)+\tau_{k}(1+\tau_{k}^{-2})\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}.

Now assume that
\tau_{k}>n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}
. In this case, we have
\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j}=\tau_{k}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-\frac{d-2}{d}}
so using (
4.3
) and Proposition
16
again yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{X^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\Bigg[\tau_{k}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-\frac{d-2}{d}}\int_{\tau_{k,j}}^{\tau_{k,j+1}}\int\|\widehat{s}(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{2}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-2\frac{d-2}{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{k}^{2}}\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}+\frac{1}{n}\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{k}^{2}}n^{\frac{d-2}{2\beta+d}}\right)+\frac{C}{n}(1+\tau_{k}^{-2})\log(n)^{C_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-2\frac{d-2}{d}}n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}.

Finally coming back to (
4.3
), taking
{k^{\star}}:=\max\{k|\tau_{k}\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}\}
, we conclude the proof by the bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(\widehat{p},p^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1}+C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{k^{\star}}\left(n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}\right)^{1/2}+C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{k=k^{\star}}^{m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\Upsilon_{k}-1}\left((\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-2\frac{d-2}{d}}n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{2\beta+d}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}},

where we used that
k^{*}\leq m\leq C\log n
, together with

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{k}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-\frac{d-2}{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\left((\tau_{k}n)^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}+1\right)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}(\tau_{k}n^{\frac{2}{2\beta+d}})^{-\frac{d-2}{d}}\leq 2^{\frac{d-2}{2}}

for all
k\geq k^{*}
.
∎

5
Final rates and outlook

5.1
Minimax optimality of SGMs

This section presents the final convergence rates achieved under our statistical model.
Assuming that the data distribution
p^{\star}
satisfies Assumption
1
, the associated score function enjoys both the one-sided Lipschitz regularity property (
B
) and the higher-order regularity properties (
C
), (
D
).
By applying Lemma
13
, we then directly conclude that the score-based estimator achieves the minimax optimal rate.

Theorem I

(Main minimax upper bound)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
,
\beta>0
,
K\geq 1
,
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}
(see (
41
)) and
(X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)})
an i.i.d. sample drawn from
p^{\star}
.
Then when using the score estimator (
39
) with the neural network class (
43
), the distribution of
\widehat{p}
of the score-based generator
\widehat{X}_{\overline{T}-\underline{T}}
obtained from the backward dynamic (
37
) with
K^{-1}\leq\sigma\leq K
and
\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\overline{T}-\underline{T}])}\leq K
, satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}}\ \mathbb{E}_{X^{(i)}\sim p^{\star}}[\mathrm{W}_{1}(p^{\star},\widehat{p})]\underset{{\mathrm{polylog}(n)}}{\lesssim}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Proof.

From Lemma
9
we have that if
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}
, then the score function
s^{\star}(t,x)
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.
Applying Lemma
13
, we obtain the minimax upper bound.
∎

The rate
n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}
naturally arises in the estimation of a
\beta
-smooth density in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
in transportation distances.
It is known to be the minimax rate for estimating densities in
\mathcal{H}_{K}^{\beta}
with respect to the Wasserstein-1 distance
Uppal et al. 2019
.
In fact, a close look at the proof of
(
Uppal et al. 2019
, Theorem 4)
shows that this minimax rate still holds when densities are further restricted to have support contained in a fixed bounded set.
What remains is to verify that our additional shape constraint does not simplify the estimation problem. This is the focus of the next result.

Theorem II

(Main minimax lower bound)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
,
\beta>0
,
K\geq 1
,
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}
(see (
41
)) and
(X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)})
be an i.i.d. sample drawn from
p^{\star}
. Then,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{p}_{n}}\sup_{p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}}\ \mathbb{E}_{X^{(i)}\sim p^{\star}}[\mathrm{W}_{1}(p^{\star},\widehat{p}_{n})]\gtrsim n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}},

where
\widehat{p}_{n}
ranges among all the estimators of
p^{\star}
based on
(X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)})
.

The proof of Theorem
II
builds on the argument developed in
(
Uppal et al. 2019
, Theorem 4)
, with minor adaptations to account precisely for the model
\mathcal{A}^{\beta}_{K}
.
For completeness, these modifications are detailed in Section
B
.

5.2
Perspectives

Overall, our results contribute to the theoretical understanding of score-based generative models by providing a streamlined proof strategy and opening new directions for generalization. In particular, we demonstrate that regularity of the score function is sufficient to achieve minimax convergence rates, and that such regularity naturally arises under broad conditions on the data distribution. Remarkably, it suffices for the score to exhibit high-order Hölder regularity on high-probability sets, up to logarithmic factors. This observation suggests that Hölder smoothness may not be the most intrinsic notion of regularity to control; instead, the score function might possess Sobolev-type regularity with respect to the law
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}
.

This perspective gives hope that regularity results for score functions could extend to settings where the data distribution lies near lower-dimensional structures, such as manifolds. In particular future work, could be dedicated to study the following conjecture.

Conjecture 17
.

Let
P^{\star}
be a probability measure with density
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{K}
with respect to the volume measure of a
\mathcal{C}^{\beta+1}
compact submanifold with reach bounded below. If the density of
p^{\star}
is bounded away from zero, the score function associated to
p^{\star}
satisfies, for all

,

s^{\star}(t,\cdot)\in W^{\beta+\gamma,2}_{Ct^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern where
W^{\beta+\gamma,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern
