0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t})
stands for the
L^{2}(p_{t})
-Sobolev space of regularity
\beta+\gamma
.

The intuition behind the conjecture is that as
t\to 0
, the measure
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}
concentrates around the support of
p^{\star}
, and the score function
s^{\star}(t,x)
becomes increasingly regular as
x

is closer to the manifold.

If the regularity results from
Stéphanovitch 2025
could be extended to the manifold setting, they would significantly simplify existing proofs of statistical optimality (see Section
1.2.1
) by leveraging the fact that the score adapts not only to the intrinsic dimension of the data distribution but also to its regularity. We believe that pushing further the analysis of space-time regularity of the score function could lead to a better theoretical understanding of the impressive performance of score-based generative models.

Appendix A
Details for the proof of Theorem
I

A.1
Proof of Proposition
5

Proof of Proposition
5
.

By assumption, there exists
C_{A}>0
such that for all
x\notin\mathrm{B}(0,C_{A})
we have that
-\nabla^{2}\log(q(x))\succeq(2A)^{-1}I_{d\times d}
,
-\langle\nabla\log(q(x)),x\rangle\geq(2A)^{-1}\|x\|^{2}
and
-\log(q(x))\geq(4A)^{-1}\|x\|^{2}
. Let
l:[-1,C_{A}+1]\rightarrow[0,1]
be a smooth non-decreasing function such that
l([-1,0])=0
and
l([C_{A},C_{A}+1])=1
. Defining,

\displaystyle u(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(4A)^{-1}\|x\|^{2}&\text{ if }\|x\|\leq C_{A}\\
l(\|x\|-C_{A})(-\log(q(x)))+(1-l(\|x\|-C_{A}))(4A)^{-1}\|x\|^{2}&\text{ if }C_{A}\leq\|x\|\leq 2C_{A}\\
-\log(q(x))&\text{ if }2C_{A}\leq\|x\|,\end{array}\right.

one can check that
u
satisfies
(4A)^{-1}I_{d\times d}\preceq\nabla^{2}u(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
straightforwardly.
Then, defining

\displaystyle b(x):=\log(q(x))+u(x)+a(x),

we have that
b
satisfies Assumption
1
-1. Therefore, we conclude that
p(x)=q(x)e^{a(x)}=e^{-u(x)+b(x)}
satisfies Assumption
1
.

∎

A.2
Proof of Proposition
6

Proof of Proposition
6
.

Let us write
x^{\star}:=\argmin_{x}u(x)
. From the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle\|s^{\star}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla s^{\star}(t,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\|x-x^{\star}\|+\|s^{\star}(t,x^{\star})\|.

(120)

Now, writing
\varphi^{t,x}
for the probability density of
\mathcal{N}(e^{-t}x,(1-e^{-2t})\sigma^{2}I_{d\times d})
, and taking
r(y):=p^{\star}(y)/\varphi^{\infty,0}(y)
, define the probability density

p^{t,x}(y):=\frac{r(y)\varphi^{t,x}(y)}{\int r(z)\varphi^{t,x}(z)\mathrm{d}z}.

From
(
Stéphanovitch 2025
, Proposition 4)
, we have for all
x^{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

s^{\star}(t,x^{{}^{\prime}})=-x^{{}^{\prime}}+\frac{e^{-t}}{1-e^{-2t}}\int(y-e^{-t}x^{{}^{\prime}})p^{t,x^{{}^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{d}y)

and

\nabla s^{\star}(t,x^{{}^{\prime}})=-I_{d\times d}+\frac{e^{-2t}}{(1-e^{-2t})^{2}}\int\left(y-\int zp^{t,x^{{}^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{d}z)\right)^{\otimes 2}p^{t,x^{{}^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{d}y)-\frac{e^{-2t}}{1-e^{-2t}}I_{d\times d}.

(121)

As,

\displaystyle\int r(z)\varphi^{t,x^{\star}}(z)\mathrm{d}z\geq\int_{B(x^{\star},\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}})}r(z)\varphi^{t,x^{\star}}(z)\mathrm{d}z\geq C^{-1},

and
\|x^{\star}\|\leq C
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|s^{\star}(t,x^{\star})\|

\displaystyle=\|-x^{\star}+\frac{e^{-t}}{1-e^{-2t}}\int(y-e^{-t}x^{\star})p^{t,x^{\star}}(\mathrm{d}y)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+t^{-1}\int\|y-e^{-t}x^{\star}\|r(y)\varphi^{t,x^{\star}}(y)\mathrm{d}y\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+t^{-1}).

On the other hand, we have from (
121
) that

\inf_{x^{{}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\min}(\nabla s^{\star}(t,x^{{}^{\prime}}))\geq-1-Ce^{-2t}(1+t^{-1}).

Putting this bound together with the estimate of Proposition
7
, we deduce that

\|\nabla s^{\star}(t,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq C(1+t^{-1}).

Therefore, we obtain from (
120
) that

\displaystyle\|s^{\star}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq C(1+(1+t^{-1})\|x-x^{\star}\|)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+t^{-1})(1+\|x\|),

which concludes the proof.
∎

A.3
Proof of Lemma
9

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Under the assumption that
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
, the score
s^{\star}(t,x)=\nabla\log\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)
is regular, in the sense that it satisfies (
A
) from Proposition
6
, (
B
) from Proposition
7
, as well as (
C
) and (
D
) from Theorem
8
. Let us now show that it satisfies points 1. and 2. of Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.

As given in
(
Stéphanovitch 2025
, Section 3.1.1)
, we have

\displaystyle A_{t}^{\varepsilon}:=\begin{cases}A_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\{y\mid u(y)\leq C(\log(\varepsilon^{-1})+K+1)^{K}\}&\text{if }t\leq C_{\star}^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{-C_{\star}}\\
A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\{y\mid\|y-y^{\star}\|\leq C\log(\varepsilon^{-1})(1+K)\}&\text{if }t>C_{\star}^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{-C_{\star}},\end{cases}

corresponding to the set given by Theorem
8
. Now by Assumption
1
.2 we have that
\mathbb{P}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon})\geq 1-\varepsilon
, so we only need to check Property
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.2.

First of all, the case
t>C_{\star}^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{-C_{\star}}
is immediate.
Now, in the case
t\leq C_{\star}^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{-C_{\star}}
, for
x\in A_{t}^{\varepsilon}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\|x-y\|\leq(KC(\log(\varepsilon^{-1})+K+1)^{K})^{-K},

we have from Assumption
1
.4 that
for all
r\in[0,1]
,

\|\nabla u(x+r(y-x))\|\leq K(1+(\log(\varepsilon^{-1})+K+1)^{K^{2}}).

Then, from Taylor expansion we deduce that

\displaystyle\|u(y)\|\leq C(\log(\varepsilon^{-1})+K+1)^{K}+C_{3}\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{C_{4}}\|x-y\|.

Therefore, there exists
C_{1}^{\star},C_{2}^{\star}>0
such that for any , if
\|x-y\|\leq(C_{1}^{\star})^{-1}\log\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{-C_{2}^{\star}},
we have

\displaystyle\|u(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq C(\log(\varepsilon^{-2})+K+1)^{K}

so
y\in A_{t}^{\varepsilon^{2}}
.
We can then conclude that for
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
, the score function satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.
∎

A.4
Proof of Lemma
10

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Given an initial condition
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|X_{\overline{\tau}}\|\mid X_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\|x+\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}a_{t}(X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\|\mid X_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|+(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})V+\sqrt{d}M.

On the other hand, by the
L
-subGaussian property of
p_{\underline{\tau}}
, we have that for all
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{B}\left(0,\sqrt{L\log(1/\varepsilon)}\right)^{c}}\|x\|p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\sqrt{L\log(1/\varepsilon)}}^{\infty}\delta\mathbb{P}(\|X_{\underline{\tau}}\|\geq\delta)\mathrm{d}\delta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\sqrt{L\log(1/\varepsilon)}}^{\infty}\delta e^{-\delta^{2}/L}\mathrm{d}\delta=L\varepsilon/2,

with same expression for
\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}
.
As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{\tau}},\widetilde{p}_{\overline{\tau}})

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\int\mathbb{E}\Big[f(X_{\overline{\tau}})\mid X_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int\mathbb{E}\Big[f(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\tau}})|\widetilde{X}_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1},f(0)=0}\int\mathbb{E}\Big[f(X_{\overline{\tau}})\mid X_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big](p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}(x))\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|X_{\overline{\tau}}\|\mid X_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]\big|p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}(x)|\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\|x\|+(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})V+\sqrt{d}M)|p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}(x)|(\mathds{1}_{x\in\mathrm{B}(0,\sqrt{L\log(\varepsilon^{-1})})}+\mathds{1}_{x\notin\mathrm{B}(0,\sqrt{L\log(\varepsilon^{-1})})})\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\bigl((\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})V+\sqrt{d}M\bigr)\|p_{\underline{\tau}}-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}\|_{L^{1}}+\bigl(\sqrt{L\log(\varepsilon^{-1})}\|p_{\underline{\tau}}-\widetilde{p}_{\underline{\tau}}\|_{L^{1}}+L\varepsilon/2\bigr),

which concludes the proof.
∎

A.5
Proof of Theorem
11

Proof of Theorem
11
.

Consider the synchronous coupling given by initial condition
X_{\underline{\tau}}=\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}\sim p_{\underline{\tau}}
.
Then for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{\tau}},\bar{p}_{\overline{\tau}})

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\int f(x)\mathrm{d}p_{\overline{\tau}}(x)-\int f(x)\bar{p}_{\overline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\int\mathbb{E}\Big[f(X_{\overline{\tau}})-f(\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}})\big|X_{\underline{\tau}}=x,\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{E}\Big[\|X_{\overline{\tau}}-\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}}\|\big|X_{\underline{\tau}}=x,\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

(122)

Now, using the stochastic Itô-Alekseev-Gröbner formula
(
Hudde et al. 2024
, Theorem 1.1)
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}}-X_{\overline{\tau}}=\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\nabla\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(X_{s})(\bar{a}_{s}(X_{s})-a_{s}(X_{s}))\mathrm{d}s,

where for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the process
(\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(y))_{s}
is solution to the equation

\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(y)=y+\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{a}_{t}(\bar{X}_{t,\overline{\tau}}(y))\mathrm{d}t+\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}r_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

Because
\bar{a}
is locally Lipschitz,
\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}
is differentiable and

\displaystyle\nabla\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(y)=I_{d\times d}+\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\nabla\bar{a}_{t}(\bar{X}_{t,\overline{\tau}}(y))\bar{X}_{t,\overline{\tau}}(y)\mathrm{d}t.

Given a vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, consider
R(s):=\|\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v\|^{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}R(s)

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v,-\partial_{s}\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v,\nabla\bar{a}_{\overline{\tau}-s}(\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}})\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{a}_{\overline{\tau}-s}(\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}-s,\overline{\tau}}))R(s)

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{\overline{\tau}-s}R(s).

Since
R(0)=\|\nabla\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau},\overline{\tau}}(y)v\|^{2}=\|v\|^{2}
Gronwall’s inequality yields

R(s)\leq\|v\|^{2}e^{2\int_{0}^{s}\bar{L}_{\overline{\tau}-u}\mathrm{d}u},

or equivalently,

\|\nabla\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(y)v\|\leq e^{\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{L}_{u}\mathrm{d}u}\|v\|.

As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\|X_{\overline{\tau}}-\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|\nabla\bar{X}_{s,\overline{\tau}}(X_{s})\|\|(a_{s}(X_{s})-\bar{a}_{s}(X_{s})\|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}e^{\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{L}_{u}\mathrm{d}u}\|(a_{s}(X_{s})-\bar{a}_{s}(X_{s})\|\mathrm{d}s,

which combined with (
A.5
)
yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(p_{\overline{\tau}},\bar{p}_{\overline{\tau}})

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{E}\Big[\|X_{\overline{\tau}}-\bar{X}_{\overline{\tau}}\|\big|X_{\underline{\tau}}=x,\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}e^{\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{L}_{u}\mathrm{d}u}\|a_{s}(X_{s})-\bar{a}_{s}(X_{s})\|\mathrm{d}s\big|X_{\underline{\tau}}=x,\bar{X}_{\underline{\tau}}=x\Big]p_{\underline{\tau}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\int e^{\int_{s}^{\overline{\tau}}\bar{L}_{u}\mathrm{d}u}\|a_{s}(X_{s})-\bar{a}_{s}(X_{s})\|p_{s}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

∎

A.6
Proof of Proposition
12

The proof of Proposition
12
is based on the following deviation result.

Proposition 18

(A clipped deviation bound for empirical risk minimizers)
.

Let
p^{\star}
be a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
A_{n}
be such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{p^{\star}}(A_{n})\geq 1-n^{-4}.

For a class of function
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we let
c_{n}
and
v
be such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in A_{n},f\in\mathcal{F}}|f(x)|\leq c_{n},\text{\vskip 20.44434pt and \vskip 20.44434pt}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f^{2}(X)\leq v.

For all
f\in\mathcal{F}
, we denote by
f_{c}
its clipped version, that is

\displaystyle f_{c}(x):=\mathrm{sg}(f(x))(|f(x)|\wedge c_{n}).

For
\varepsilon>0
, recall that
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)
denote the covering number of
\mathcal{F}
at scale
\varepsilon
, in terms of sup-norm. Let
X^{(n)},\ldots,X^{(n)}
be an i.i.d. sample drawn from
p^{\star}
, and let

\displaystyle\bar{f}

\displaystyle\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f(X)),

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}(f),

where
\mathbb{E}_{n}(f)
denotes expectation of
f
with respect to sample distribution.
Then, for any
t
,
\varepsilon
and
\delta>0
, with probability larger than
1-n^{-3}-2e^{-t}
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)+\delta(\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})+\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}_{c}))+2\sqrt{v}n^{-2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon))\right)}{n}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2}.

Moreover, it holds

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)-\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}_{c}))\right]\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})\\
+6\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon))\vee n\right)}{n}\quad+5\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2},

where
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}
denotes the expectation with respect to the randomness of data
\mathbb{X}^{(n)}=\{X^{(1)},\ldots,X^{(n)}\}
.

The proof of Proposition
18
is postponed to Section
A.6.1
.
As in
Schmidt-Hieber 2020
and
Oko et al. 2023
, the proof of Proposition
18
relies on Bernstein’s deviation inequality, combined with a chaining argument.

In the particular context of denoising score matching with
p^{\star}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
, Proposition
12
will use the following setup.
Take
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{H}_{V}^{0}
, and let
s\in\mathcal{S}
.
Fix time horizons
n^{-1}\leq\underline{\tau}<\overline{\tau}
.
Recall that the denoising score matching contrast
\gamma_{\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}}(s,x)
is defined by (
38
).
Define the excess contrast by

\displaystyle\nu(s,x):=\gamma_{\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}}(s,x)-\gamma_{\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}}(s^{\star},x).

(123)

According to Theorem
3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}[\nu(s,X)]=\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|s(t,x)-s^{\star}(t,x)\|^{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}_{\!t}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

so that
s^{\star}
minimizes the expected excess contrast over all measurable functions
s:[\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
To prove Proposition
12
, a relevant strategy is then to apply Proposition
18
to the family functions
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}}=\{\nu(s,.)\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
.
This leads us to upper bound their variances and sup-norms — the objective of the next lemma.

Lemma 19
.

Assume that
p^{\star}
is
K
-subGaussian, and that
s^{\star}
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.
Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{H}_{V}^{0}
,
\sigma>0
, and
\nu(s,x)
be defined as in (
123
). Then there exists
A_{n}\subset\mathrm{B}(0,C\log(n)^{C})
such that
\mathbb{P}_{p^{\star}}(A_{n})\geq 1-n^{-4}
and, for any
\underline{\tau}\geq n^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S},x\in A_{n}}|\nu(s,x)|\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V),

where
C
depends on
K
and
d
only and
g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)=1+V^{2}+(1+\sigma+{\sigma^{-1}})^{4}(1+\underline{\tau}^{-1})+\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-2}
. In addition,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu^{2}(s,X)\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})^{2}g^{2}(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V),

where
C
depends only on
K
. Moreover, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu_{c}^{2}(s,X)\leq c_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(s,X)+c_{n}^{2}n^{-4},

where
c_{n}=C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)
, for
C
large enough depending on
K
.

The proof of Lemma
19
is postponed to Section
A.6.2
. Equipped with Proposition
18
and Lemma
19
, we are in position to prove Proposition
12
.

Proof of Proposition
12
.

Recall that the centered contrast function
\nu
is defined by (
123
). Let us denote by
c_{n}:=C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)
, for
C
large enough depending on
K
. Applying Proposition
18
to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}}:=\{\nu(s,.)\}_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
with
\delta=1/2
leads to

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu(\widehat{s},X))-\nu(\bar{s},X))-(1/2)\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(\widehat{s},X))\right]\\
\leq(1/2)\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(\bar{s},X))+c_{n}\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\vee n\right)}{n}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2}.

Using the connection between
\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(s,X)
and
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(s,X)
provided by Lemma
19
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)},X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\widehat{s},X)

\displaystyle\leq(1/2)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)},X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\widehat{s},X)+(3/2)\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\bar{s},X)+c_{0}c_{n}\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\vee n\right)}{n}

\displaystyle\quad+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2},

for some constant
c_{0}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)},X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\widehat{s},X)\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\bar{s},X)+c_{0}c_{n}\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\vee n\right)}{n}+8\varepsilon+32\varepsilon^{2}.

(124)

It remains to connect the covering numbers of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}}
with that of
\mathcal{S}
.
To do so, let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d\times d})
. We note that for all
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, definition (
123
) and a direct development of (
38
) yield

\displaystyle|\nu(s,x)-\nu(s^{\prime},x)|

\displaystyle=|\gamma(s,x)-\gamma(s^{\prime},x)|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}\|s(t,e^{-\lambda t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{Z}\|s^{\prime}(t,e^{-\lambda t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2})\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left\langle(s-s^{\prime})(t,e^{-\lambda t}x+\sigma_{t}Z),(s+s^{\prime})(t,e^{-\lambda t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)+(2Z/\sigma_{t})\right\rangle\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|s-s^{\prime}\|_{\infty}(2V+2\sqrt{d}/\sigma_{t})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}(V+\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-1})(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\|s-s^{\prime}\|_{\infty},

where we used that
(\|s\|_{\infty}\vee\|s^{\prime}\|_{\infty})\leq V
and
t\mapsto\sigma_{t}
is non-increasing. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{S}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\varepsilon)\leq\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{S},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon).

Choosing
\varepsilon:=g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})/n
in (
124
) yields, for
n
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)},X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\widehat{s},X)\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu(\bar{s},X)+C_{K}(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C_{K}}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{S},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},n^{-1})\vee n\right)}{n},

and hence the result.
∎

A.6.1
Proof of Proposition
18

To begin with the proof of Proposition
18
, a lemma connecting clipped and non-clipped expectation is needed.

Lemma 20
.

Let
p^{\star}
be a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
A_{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be such that
\mathbb{P}_{p^{\star}}(A_{n}^{c})\leq n^{-4}
, and

\sup_{x\in A_{n},f\in\mathcal{F}}|f(x)|\leq c_{n}
,
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f^{2}(X)\leq v
.

Then, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f(X)-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f_{c}(X)\right|\leq\sqrt{v}n^{-2}.

Proof of Lemma
20
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{F}
. We decompose
\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f(X)-f_{c}(X))\right|
as follows.

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f(X)-f_{c}(X))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f-f_{c})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}(X)\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f-f_{c})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X)\right|.

According to the definition of
c_{n}
,
f(X)\mathbbm{1}_{X\in A_{n}}=f_{c}(X)\mathbbm{1}_{X\in A_{n}}
, so that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f(X)-f_{c}(X))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f-f_{c})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\left|f\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X)\right|)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}_{p^{\star}}(A_{n}^{c})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f^{2}(X)}n^{-2},

where we used
|f(x)-f_{c}(x)|\leq|f(x)|
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
∎

We are now in position to prove Proposition
18
.

Proof of Proposition
18
.

Let
\widehat{f}\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}(f)
and
\bar{f}\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f(X)
. Then it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})\widehat{f}(X)+\mathbb{E}_{n}\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})\widehat{f}(X)+\mathbb{E}_{n}\bar{f}(X)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)+(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})\widehat{f}(X)+(\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}})\bar{f}(X).

(125)

We let
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon)\subset\mathcal{F}
be a minimal
\varepsilon
-covering of
\mathcal{F}
in sup norm, with cardinal
|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon)|=\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)
.
For
f\in\mathcal{F}
, let
f_{\varepsilon}(f)\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon)
denote any function such that
\|f-f_{\varepsilon}(f)\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon
.

Let us bound the first deviation term of (
A.6.1
). Straightforward computation entails that

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})\widehat{f}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{\varepsilon}(\widehat{f})+2\varepsilon.

(126)

Next, denote by
A
the event
\left\{\forall i,\;X^{(i)}\in A_{n}\right\}
, that has probability larger than
1-n^{-3}
. For a fixed
f_{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon)
, we let
f_{j,c}
denote its clipped version.
On
A
it holds
\mathbb{E}_{n}(f_{j})=\mathbb{E}_{n}(f_{j,c})
. Since Lemma
20
entails that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f_{j}(X)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f_{j,c}(X)+\sqrt{v}n^{-2},

on
A
it holds

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j}(X)\leq(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j,c}(X)+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}.

(127)

Applying a standard Bernstein inequality (see, e.g,
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Corollary 2.11)
)
f_{j,c}
leads to

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j,c}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{j,c})t/n}+c_{n}t/n

\displaystyle\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{j,c})+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t}{n},

with probability larger than
1-2e^{-t}
.

Combining the above calculation with (
127
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\cap\left\{|(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j}(X)|\geq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{j,c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t}{n}\right\}\right]\leq 2e^{-t}.

Recalling that
|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\mathcal{F},\varepsilon)|=\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)
, a standard union bound yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\cap\left\{\exists j\mid|(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j}|\geq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{j,c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}\right\}\right]\leq 2e^{-t}.

(128)

Denoting by
B_{t,n}
the event

\displaystyle B_{t,n}=\left\{\forall j\quad|(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{j}|\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{j,c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}\right\},

it comes
\mathbb{P}(B_{t,n}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(B_{t,n}^{c}\cap A)+\mathbb{P}(A^{c})\leq n^{-3}+2e^{-t}
. Furthermore, on
B_{t,n}
, (
126
) entails

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})\widehat{f}

\displaystyle\quad\leq(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}-\mathbb{E}_{n})f_{\varepsilon}(\widehat{f})+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{\varepsilon}(\widehat{f})_{c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}_{c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}+2\varepsilon+8\varepsilon^{2},

(129)

where we used that, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(f_{c})

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}((f_{\varepsilon}(f))_{c})+8\|f_{c}-(f_{\varepsilon}(f))_{c}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}((f_{\varepsilon}(f))_{c})+8\varepsilon^{2}.

Using the same computation as above for
(\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}})\bar{f}
leads to

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}})\bar{f}\leq 2\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})+\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}+2\varepsilon+8\varepsilon^{2},

(130)

on
B_{t,n}
. Plugging (
A.6.1
) and (
130
) into (
A.6.1
) yields that, still on
B_{t,n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)+2\delta(\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})+\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}})(\widehat{f}_{c})+2\sqrt{v}n^{-2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{2\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2},

that gives the deviation bound of Proposition
18
, replacing
2\delta
by
\delta
.

For the expectation bound, Jensen inequality entails
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}f(X)\leq\sqrt{v}
, for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Using the deviation bound from above, we may write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)-\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}))\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}\left(\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}(X))-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)-\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{B_{t,n}}\right)+\sqrt{v}(n^{-3}+2e^{-t})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})+2\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+2\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\frac{t+\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\right)}{n}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{v}(n^{-3}+2e^{-t}).

Choosing
t=2\log(n)
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{X}^{(n)}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\widehat{f}(X)-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\bar{f}(X)-\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\widehat{f}))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\mathrm{Var}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\bar{f}_{c})+6\left(c_{n}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\frac{\log\left(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\varepsilon)\vee n\right)}{n}

\displaystyle\quad+5\sqrt{v}n^{-2}+4\varepsilon+16\varepsilon^{2},

that is the expectation bound of Proposition
18
.
∎

A.6.2
Proof of Lemma
19

Proof of Lemma
19
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\|s\|_{\infty}\leq V
. Then

\displaystyle\gamma_{\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau}}(s,x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|s(t,e^{-\lambda t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\|Z\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2V^{2}(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})+2d\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})(V^{2}+\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-2})),

since
t\mapsto\sigma_{t}^{-2}
is non-increasing.

Since
s^{\star}
satisfies Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\left(A_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-4},

for
\varepsilon_{n}=n^{-C}
and
A_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
onto which Theorem
8
is valid.
For short, write

t_{n}:=C(1+\sigma)^{-2}\log(n)^{-C_{+}},

for a constant
C_{+}
that will be chosen large enough.
We will show that, for any
0<t\leq t_{n}
,
x\in A_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
and
\|z\|\leq 4\log(n)
,
xe^{-t}+\sigma_{t}z\in A_{0}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}
, for
n
large enough.

A standard triangle inequality yields that

\displaystyle\|x-e^{-t}x-\sigma_{t}z\|

\displaystyle\leq(1-e^{-t})\|x\|+\sigma_{t}\|z\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)t+C\sigma\sqrt{t}\log n

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma)\sqrt{t}\log n,

for
t\leq 1
and
n
large enough. According to Properties
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
,

\displaystyle C(1+\sigma)\sqrt{t}\log n\leq C^{-1}\log(\varepsilon^{-1}_{n})^{-C}=C\log(n)^{-C}

is thus enough to guarantee that
x-e^{-t}x-\sigma_{t}z\in A_{0}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}
. Choosing
C_{+}
large enough in
t_{n}=C(1+\sigma)^{-2}\log(n)^{-C_{+}}
ensures that the above inequality is satisfied, for any
t\leq t_{n}
, and
n
large enough.

We are now in position to control
\gamma(s^{\star},x)
, for
x\in A_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
.
According to Proposition
6
and Theorem
8
, it holds

•

if
x\in A_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
,
\|s^{\star}(t,x)\|\leq C\log(\varepsilon^{-1})^{C}(1+t^{-1/2}+{\sigma^{-2}})
;

•

if
x\notin A_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
,
\|s^{\star}(t,x)\|\leq C\|x\|(1+t^{-1})
.

For short we denote by
A_{n}=A_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
, and
A^{\prime}_{n}=A_{0}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}\cap A_{\infty}^{\varepsilon^{2}_{n}}
, for
\varepsilon_{n}
defined above. Note that, according to Theorem
8
,
A_{n}\subset\mathrm{B}(0,C\log(n)^{C})
.

If
n^{-1}\leq t\leq t_{n}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\|^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\log(n)^{C}(t^{-1}+{\sigma^{-4}})\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\mathbbm{1}_{e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\in A^{\prime}_{n}})+Ct^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\notin A^{\prime}_{n}})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\log(n)^{C}(t^{-1}+{\sigma^{-4}})+Ct^{-2}\log(n)^{C}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|>4\log(n))+Ct^{-2}\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|Z\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\|Z\|\geq 4\log(n)})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(1+\sigma^{2}+{\sigma^{-4}})(\log(n)^{C}t^{-1}+n^{-2}t^{-2})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(1+\sigma^{2}+{\sigma^{-4}})\log(n)^{C}t^{-1},

using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
tn\geq 1
for the last lines.

If
t\geq t_{n}=c\log(n)^{-c}(1+\sigma)^{-2}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\|^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+t^{-2})\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\|^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma)^{2}\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-2})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma)^{4}\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-1}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\gamma(s^{\star},x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z\|^{2})\mathrm{d}t+2d\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma+{\sigma^{-1}})^{4}\log(n)^{C}(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})(1+\underline{\tau}^{-1})+2d(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-2}.

It immediatly follows that

\displaystyle|\nu(s,x)|\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V),

with
g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)=1+V^{2}+(1+\sigma+{\sigma^{-1}})^{4}(1+\underline{\tau}^{-1})+\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-2}
.

Now we turn to the bound on
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu^{2}(s,X)
. For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the above calculation yields that

\displaystyle\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}z)\|^{4}\leq C\left(\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-2}+{\sigma^{-8}})+(\|x\|^{4}+\sigma^{4}\|z\|^{4})(1+t^{-4})\right).

Thus, for
t\geq n^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}(X)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma+{\sigma^{-1}})^{8}\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-2})

\displaystyle\quad+C\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-2}+{\sigma^{-8}})n^{-4}+C(1+t^{-4})\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\|X\|^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X))+C\sigma^{4}(1+t^{-4})n^{-4}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sigma+\sigma^{-1})^{8}\log(n)^{C}(1+t^{-2}),

using that
p^{\star}
is
K
-subGaussian, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
tn\geq 1
.

We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\gamma^{2}(s^{\star},X)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\left(\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star},Z}\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{4}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})^{2}\sigma_{\underline{\tau}}^{-4}+C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})^{2}C(1+\sigma+{\sigma^{-1}})^{8}\log(n)^{C}(1+\underline{\tau}^{-2}).

At last,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu^{2}(s,X)

\displaystyle\leq 2(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\gamma^{2}(s,X)+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\gamma^{2}(s^{\star},X))

\displaystyle\leq C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})^{2}\log(n)^{C}g^{2}(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V).

Let us turn to the last inequality of Lemma
19
.
For all
x,t,z
, it holds

\displaystyle\left|\|s(t,e^{t}x+\sigma_{t}z)-z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}-\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}z)-z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|(s-s^{\star})((t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}z)\|\left(\|s(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}z)-z/\sigma_{t}\|+\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}z)-z/\sigma_{t}\|\right).

Using Cauchy-Schwaz inequality entails that

\displaystyle|\nu(s,x)|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|s(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|(s-s^{\star})((t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)\|\left(\|s(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|+\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|(s-s^{\star})((t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}(\|s(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2}+\|s^{\star}(t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)-Z/\sigma_{t}\|^{2})\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|(s-s^{\star})((t,e^{-t}x+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t}\sqrt{2(\gamma(s,x)+\gamma(s^{\star},x))}.

Let us decompose
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}(X))+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X)).

Since
\nu_{c}(s,X)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{B}(0,A_{n})}(X)=\nu(s,X)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{B}(0,A_{n})}(X)
, the above calculation yields the following bound for the first term:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}(X))

\displaystyle\leq c_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\int_{\underline{\tau}}^{\overline{\tau}}\|(s-s^{\star})((t,e^{-t}X+\sigma_{t}Z)\|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad=c_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu(s,X)),

for
\left|2(\gamma(s,x)+\gamma(s^{\star},x))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{B}(0,c_{0}K\log(n)}(x)\right|\leq c_{n}=C(\overline{\tau}-\underline{\tau})\log(n)^{C}g(\underline{\tau},\sigma,V)
, using the bounds on
\gamma(s,x)
and
\gamma(s^{\star},x)
provided above and Theorem
3
for the last line. In turn, the second term may be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(\nu_{c}(s,X)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}^{c}}(X))

\displaystyle\leq c_{n}^{2}\mathbb{P}_{X\sim p^{\star}}(A_{n}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq c_{n}^{2}/n^{4}.

∎

A.7
Proof of Proposition
16

A.7.1
Preliminaries and plan for the proof

Let
C_{1}^{\star}>0
and define for all
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,m-1\}
the set

\mathcal{B}_{k}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}|\ \mathrm{dist}(x,A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n})\leq\log(n)^{-C_{1}^{\star}}\}.

(131)

By Property
(\mathcal{P}_{\beta})
.2, taking
C_{1}^{\star}>0
large enough we have that

\mathcal{B}_{k}\subset A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n^{2}}.

(132)

In order to prove Proposition
16
, let us define a smooth approximation of the projection on the subset
\mathcal{B}_{k}
. This is possible as the boundary of
\mathcal{B}_{k}
is
C^{\infty}
as the set
A_{t}^{\varepsilon}
is convex.
Let
\pi_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for all
x\in A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}
we have
\pi_{k}(x)=x
and for all
y\notin\mathcal{B}_{k}
we have
\pi_{k}(y)=\pi_{\mathcal{B}_{k}}(y)
the orthogonal projection on
\mathcal{B}_{k}
. In particular we can choose
\pi_{k}
such that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we have

\|\nabla^{i}\pi_{k}(x)\|\leq C_{i}\log(n)^{-C_{\star}^{1}(i-1)}.

(133)

To avoid the bad behavior of
s^{\star}(\tau_{k},\cdot)
outside of
\mathcal{B}_{k}
, we are going to approximate the function

x\mapsto s^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))+\pi_{k}(x)

(134)

on the set

\mathcal{B}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}|\ \mathrm{dist}(x,\mathcal{B}_{k})\leq\log(n)^{C_{2}^{\star}}\},

(135)

with
C_{2}^{\star}>0
to be fixed later such that for all
t>0
we have
A_{t}^{1/n^{2}}\subset\mathcal{B}.
For lightness of notation, we define

S^{\star}(t,x):=s^{\star}(t,x)+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}x,

(136)

which is the function satisfying the regularity properties of Section
2.3
.

Let us fix
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,m-1\}
and take
t\in[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}].
For
l\geq 0
, we define
P^{\lfloor l\rfloor}
the
\lfloor l\rfloor
-th Taylor approximation which given an input
(a_{q})_{q=0}^{\lfloor l\rfloor}
, outputs the polynomial

P^{\lfloor l\rfloor}\big((a_{q})_{q=0}^{\lfloor l\rfloor}\big)(t)=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor l\rfloor}a_{q}\frac{(t-\tau_{k})^{q}}{q!}.

In particular, for a function
f\in\mathcal{H}^{l}_{B}
we have

|f(t)-P^{\lfloor l\rfloor}\big((f^{(q)}(\tau_{k}))_{q=0}^{\lfloor l\rfloor}\big)(t)|\leq B|t-\tau_{k}|^{l}.

Using these Taylor approximations, we are going to build a polynomial function
s_{N}:[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}}\|S^{\star}(t,x)-s_{N}(t,x)\|\leq\frac{C}{\tau_{k}}\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}},

and then show that there exists a neural network
\bar{s}\in\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})_{d}^{d+1}
such that

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}}\|s_{N}(t,x)-\bar{s}(t,x)\|\leq n^{-1}.

We treat separately the cases
\tau_{k}\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}
and
n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}<\tau_{k}
to exploit the increasing regularity of
s^{\star}(t,\cdot)
as
t
becomes larger.

A.7.2
Proof of Proposition
16
in the case
t\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}

Approximation with polynomials of neural nets

Recalling the definition of
S^{\star}
in (
136
) and using the regularity property (
D
) we have that for all
q\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}

\|\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(t,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1}(A_{t}^{1/n^{2}})}\leq Ce^{-t}\left(1+t^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}(1+\log(n)^{C_{2}})\right)

so from (
132
) we deduce that

\|\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1}(\mathcal{B})}\leq Ce^{-t}\left(1+t^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}(1+\log(n)^{C_{2}})\right).

Then, taking
\varepsilon:=\left(\tau_{k}^{-1/d}n^{\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{-1}
and
q\in\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor\}
, from Proposition
15
we have that there exists a
\tanh
neural network

N_{k}^{q}\in\Psi\big(2,C(\log(n)^{C_{2}+C_{2}^{\star}}\varepsilon^{-1})^{d},n^{C_{2}},C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}-(q-\frac{\beta}{2})\vee 0},\infty\big)^{d}_{d}

such that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))-N_{k}^{q}(x)\|\leq Ce^{-t}\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}\varepsilon^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1}

(137)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))-\nabla N_{k}^{q}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-t}\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}\varepsilon^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1-1}.

(138)

For
t\in[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define the Taylor approximation of
S^{\star}(\cdot,\pi_{k}(x))
with respect to time by

\displaystyle s_{N}(t,x)=P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big((N_{k}^{q}(x))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t).

(139)

Then, for
x\in\mathcal{B}
we have

\displaystyle\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)\|

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\|P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)-s_{N}(t,x)\|.

Using the time-regularity property (
C
) we have

\displaystyle\|S^{\star}(\cdot,\pi_{k}(x))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{(\beta+1)/2}([t,\infty))}\leq C\left(1+t^{-1}(1+\log(n)^{C_{2}})\right),

which gives for
x\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big((\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}(t-\tau_{k})^{(\beta+1)/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

On the other hand, using (
137
) we have

\displaystyle\|P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)-s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))-N_{k}^{q}(x))(t-\tau_{k})^{q}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\tau_{k}^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}\left(\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1}(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k})^{q}.

Now, if
q\leq\beta/2
, recalling that
\tau_{k}^{1/2}\leq\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
we have

\displaystyle\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{\beta+1-2q}(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{\beta+1}\left(\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{-2q}(2\tau_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

On the other hand, if
q>\beta/2
we have

\displaystyle\tau_{k}^{-1-q+\beta/2}\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{k}^{-1+\beta/2}\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\tau_{k}^{-q}(2\tau_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

We can then conclude that for
x\in\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

(140)

Construction of the final neural net on
\mathcal{B}_{k}

Let us now show that there exists a neural network
\bar{s}\in\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d}
such that

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|s_{N}(t,x)-\bar{s}(t,x)\|\leq n^{-1}.

We know from Corollary
3.7 in
De Ryck et al. 2021
that there exists a
\tanh
neural network
P_{N}:\mathbb{R}^{d\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with depth
O(1)
, width
O(1)
and weights bounded by
O(n^{C_{2}})
such that for all
i\in\{0,1\}
,

\sup_{y\in[-n,n]^{d\times\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor},t\in[0,n]}\|\nabla^{i}P_{N}(y,t)-\nabla^{i}P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big((y_{k})_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)\|\leq n^{-1}.

(141)

Using this approximation of polynomials, we define the
\tanh
neural network
\bar{s}_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
by

\bar{s}_{1}(t,x):=P_{N}\Big(\big(N_{k}^{q}(x)\big)_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor},t\Big).

(142)

In particular,
the width of
\bar{s}_{1}
is bounded by
O\left(\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{d}\right)
, depth by
O(1)
and weights bounded by
O(n^{C_{2}})
, so
\bar{s}_{1}

belongs to
\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},\infty,\infty)^{d+1}_{d}
and satisfies from (
141
) that

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|s_{N}(t,x)-\bar{s}_{1}(t,x)\|\leq n^{-1}.

(143)

Construction of the final neural net on
\mathbb{R}^{d}

Now, in order to have a neural net with finite
\lambda_{\max}
on the whole
\mathbb{R}^{d}
space, let us define the cut-off function

\delta(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{if }x\in\mathcal{B}_{k}\\
\mathrm{dist}(x,\mathcal{B}^{c})\Big(\mathrm{dist}(x,\mathcal{B}_{k})+\mathrm{dist}(x,\mathcal{B}^{c})\Big)^{-1}&\text{if }x\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{k}\\
0&\text{if }x\notin\mathcal{B}.\end{array}\right.

(144)

Let
\bar{\delta}
and
\bar{\times}
denote the tanh neural network approximations of
\delta
and the multiplication operation respectively, both constructed to approximate their targets with precision
n^{-1}
in the
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-norm. Then, the final neural network
\bar{s}
, defined on
[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, is given by:

\bar{s}(t,x):=\bar{\times}(\bar{\delta}(x),\bar{s}_{1}(t,x)).

(145)

In particular we have that
\bar{s}

belongs to
\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},\infty,\infty)^{d+1}_{d}
and from (
140
) and (
143
),

\sup_{t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]}\sup_{x\in A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}}\|S^{\star}(t,x)-\bar{s}(t,x)\|\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Bound on the sup-norm

By definition, for
x\notin\mathcal{B}
, we have
\|\bar{s}(t,x)\|\leq n^{-1}
. Now, for
x\in\mathcal{B}
we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{s}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{s}_{1}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{s}_{1}(t,x)-P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}((N_{k}^{q}(\cdot))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor},t))\|+\|P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}((N_{k}^{q}(\cdot))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor},t))\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}+C\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\|N_{k}^{q}(x)(t-\tau_{k})^{q}\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}+\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}-(q-\frac{\beta}{2})\vee 0}\tau_{k}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}.

Bound on
\lambda_{\max}
for
x\in\mathcal{B}_{k}

For
x\in\mathcal{B}_{k}
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{s}(t,x))

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(t,x)-\nabla s_{N}(t,x)))+\lambda_{\max}(\nabla s_{N}(t,x)-\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x))

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\lambda_{\max}(\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x))

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\|\nabla N_{k}^{q}(x)\|+\lambda_{\max}(\nabla s_{N}(t,x)-\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x))+Ce^{-t}t^{-1+\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}.

Now, for any
x\in\mathcal{B}
, using (
138
) we get

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\|\nabla N_{k}^{q}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))-\nabla N_{k}^{q}(x)\|+\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-1}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\tau_{k}^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}\varepsilon^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1-1}+\tau_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}-(q+\frac{1-\beta}{2})\vee 0}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-1}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}(\tau_{k}^{-1}\varepsilon^{\beta-2q}+\tau_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}-(q+\frac{1-\beta}{2})\vee 0})\mathds{1}_{2q<\beta}+\tau_{k}^{-1-(q-\beta/2)}\mathds{1}_{2q\geq\beta}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}(\tau_{k}^{-1/2}+\tau_{k}^{-1}(\varepsilon^{\beta-2q}+\tau_{k}^{\beta/2-q}))\mathds{1}_{2q<\beta}+\tau_{k}^{-1-(q-\beta/2)}\mathds{1}_{2q\geq\beta}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}(\nabla s_{N}(t,x)-\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x))

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)-\nabla P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(\cdot)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)(x)\|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\|\nabla P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(\cdot)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)(x)-\nabla s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)-\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)\frac{(t-\tau_{k})^{q}}{q!}\|

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}(t-\tau_{k})^{q}\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k},\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)-\nabla N_{k}^{q}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}S^{\star}(\cdot,\pi_{k}(x))\circ\nabla\pi_{k}(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2}-\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}([\tau_{k},\infty))}\tau_{k}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}\rfloor}\tau_{k}^{q}\big(\tau_{k}^{-1-((q-\beta/2)\vee 0)}\varepsilon^{(\beta+1-2q)\vee 1-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(\tau_{k}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1-((\frac{\beta+1}{2}-\beta/2)\vee 0)}+\tau_{k}^{-1+\beta/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}.

Bound on
\lambda_{\max}
for
x\notin\mathcal{B}_{k}

By construction, we have
\|\nabla\bar{s}(t,x)\|\leq n^{-1}
for all

x\notin\mathcal{B}
. Now, for
x\in\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{k}
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{s}(t,x))

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\big(\nabla\bar{s}(t,x)-\nabla(y\mapsto\bar{\delta}(y)\bar{s}_{1}(t,y))(x)\big)+\lambda_{\max}\big(\nabla(y\mapsto\bar{\delta}(y)\bar{s}_{1}(t,y))(x)\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\big(\|\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(x)\|+\|\nabla\bar{\delta}(x)\|\big)+\bar{\delta}(x)\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(x))+\|\bar{s}_{1}(x)\|\|\nabla\bar{\delta}(x)\|.

On one hand, from the precedent paragraph and property (
D
) we have

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\big(\|\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(x)-\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)\|+\|\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)\|\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\big(V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}+C\log(n)^{C_{2}}e^{-t}t^{-\big(1-\frac{\beta}{2})\vee 0\big)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, recalling the definitions of
\delta
and
\mathcal{B}
in (
144
) and (
135
) respectively, we deduce that

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\nabla\bar{\delta}(x)\|\leq Cn^{-1}\log(n)^{-C_{2}^{\star}}\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}(x)\lambda_{\max}(\nabla\bar{s}_{1}(x))+\|\bar{s}_{1}(x)\|\|\nabla\bar{\delta}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\big(\nabla\bar{s}_{1}-\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)\big)+\lambda_{\max}\big(\nabla S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(\cdot))(x)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+Ce^{-t}\log(n)^{C_{2}}t^{-1/2}\|\nabla\bar{\delta}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}+Ce^{-t}\log(n)^{C_{2}}t^{-1/2}\log(n)^{-C_{2}^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}},

taking
C_{2}^{\star}>0
large enough in (
135
).

A.7.3
Proof of Proposition
16
in the case
t>n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}

The main difference from the case
t\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}
lies in the polynomial approximation of neural networks (first paragraph of Section
A.7.2
). We therefore focus on detailing this aspect, as the remainder of the proof closely follows the arguments of Section
A.7.2
.

To obtain a more refined approximation of
S^{\star}
, this case is based on a similar construction as above, but requires an additional refinement of intervals
[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}]
into
\Upsilon_{k}
sub-intervals, as described in Section
4.1
. Furthermore, we use the additional regularity of
S^{\star}(t,\cdot)
gained from the fact that
t
is large.
Let us set
\varepsilon:=\left(\tau_{k}^{-1/d}n^{\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{-1}
,
\gamma:=(2\beta+d)/(d-2)
and
\gamma_{2}:=(\beta+1)(d-2)
. Recalling that

\tau_{k,j}:=\left(1+j/\Upsilon_{k}\right)\tau_{k}
, let
j\in\{0,\ldots,\Upsilon_{k}\}
and
q\in\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor\}
.
We let

N_{k,j}^{q}\in\Psi\big(2,C(\log(n)^{C_{2}+C_{2}^{\star}}\varepsilon^{-1})^{d},n^{C_{2}},C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}-(q-\frac{\beta}{2})\vee 0},\infty\big)^{d}_{d}

be a
\tanh
neural network given by Proposition
15
, such that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x))-N_{k,j}^{q}(x)\|\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-q-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\varepsilon^{\beta+\gamma}

and

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x))-\nabla N_{k,j}^{q}(x)\|\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-q-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\varepsilon^{\beta+\gamma-1}.

For
t\in[\tau_{k,j},\tau_{k,j+1}]
, define

\displaystyle s_{N}(t,x):=P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big((N_{k,j}^{q}(x))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big)(t).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|S^{\star}(t,\pi_{k}(x))-P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big((\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)\|

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\|P^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big((\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x)))_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\big)(t)-s_{N}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-(1+\gamma_{2})}(\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j})^{(\beta+1)/2+\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+C\|\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x))-N_{k,j}^{q}(x))(t-\tau_{k,j})^{q}\|.

Now, for the first term we have

\displaystyle\tau_{k}^{-(1+\gamma_{2})}(\tau_{k,j+1}-\tau_{k,j})^{(\beta+1)/2+\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-(1+\gamma_{2})}(\tau_{k}(\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{\frac{-2}{2\beta+d}})^{\frac{d-2}{d}})^{(\beta+1)/2+\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-(1+\gamma_{2})+\frac{2}{d}((\beta+1)/2+\gamma_{2})}n^{\frac{-(d-2)(\beta+1+2\gamma_{2})}{d(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\|\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}(\partial_{t}^{q}S^{\star}(\tau_{k,j},\pi_{k}(x))-N_{k,j}^{q}(x))(t-\tau_{k,j})^{q}\|

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\leq C\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\tau_{k}^{-q-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\tau_{k}^{1/d}n^{-\frac{d-2}{d(2\beta+d)}}\right)^{\beta+\gamma}(\tau_{k}(\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{\frac{-2}{2\beta+d}})^{\frac{d-2}{d}})^{q}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\frac{\beta+1}{2}+\gamma_{2}\rfloor}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\tau_{k}^{-1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Finally, as in the case
t\leq n^{-\frac{2}{2\beta+d}}
, we have that
s_{N}
can be approximated by a tanh neural network
\bar{s}\in\Psi(L_{k},W_{k},B_{k},V_{k},V_{k}^{{}^{\prime}})^{d+1}_{d}
up to an error
n^{-1}
on
A_{\tau_{k}}^{1/n}
, which concludes the proof.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
II

We slightly adapt the proof of
(
Donoho et al. 1996
, Theorem 2)
and
(
Uppal et al. 2019
, Theorem 4)
, using standard bayesian-like lower bound arguments comparing perturbed distributions.

Proof of Theorem
II
.

As a base density, let us choose the standard gaussian

\displaystyle g_{0}(x):=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp(-\|x\|^{2}/2).

To perturb it, let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
denote a kernel-like function such that

•

\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
;

•

\mathrm{Supp}(\Psi)\subset(-1,1)^{d}
;

•

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi(u)du=0
, and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{2}(u)du=1
.

Let us now fix a resolution
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
to be chosen later. Set
m:=2^{dj}
.
Given a multi-index
\mathbf{k}
of the grid
\{0,\ldots,2^{j}-1\}^{d}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we write

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{k}}(x):=\sqrt{m}\psi(2^{j}x-(2^{j}-1)\mathbbm{1}_{d}+2\mathbf{k}),

where
\mathbbm{1}_{d}:=(1,\ldots,1)
. Functions
\psi_{\mathbf{k}}
correspond to translations and scalings of the base perturbation
\Psi
. They cover a grid of the hypercube
[-1,1]^{d}
at scale
2^{-j}
, and are designed so as to have disjoint supports and be normalized, in the sense that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}=1
.

Given
\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}^{m}
, consider the function

g_{\varepsilon}:=g_{0}+c_{1}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}\psi_{\mathbf{k}},

for some constant
c_{1}>0
to be chosen later. Its integral is trivially equal to one.
Furthermore, the function

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\varepsilon}}{g_{0}}-1=\frac{c_{1}}{g_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}\psi_{\mathbf{k}},

has support included in
[-1,1]^{d}
, and
g_{0}\geq c>0
on this support. As a result, it holds

\displaystyle\left|\frac{g_{\varepsilon}}{g_{0}}-1\right|\leq 1/2

as soon as
c_{1}\leq c\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{-1}2^{-dj/2+1}
. Since
\log\in\mathcal{H}_{C_{\beta}}^{\lceil\beta\rceil}((1/2;3/2))
, for some constant
C_{\beta}
, and
g_{\varepsilon}/g_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{Cc_{1}2^{j(\beta+d/2)}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the Faa di Bruno formula ensures that
\log(g_{\varepsilon}/g_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{C_{\beta}c_{1}2^{j(\beta+d/2)}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Thus choosing
c_{1}:=c_{K}2^{-j(\beta+d/2)}
for
c_{K}
small enough yields that
\log(g_{\varepsilon}/g_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{C_{K}}^{\beta}
. For such a
c_{1}
, it holds
g_{\varepsilon}=g_{0}e^{a_{\varepsilon}}
, for
a_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{H}_{C_{K}}^{\beta}
. According to Proposition
5
, we deduce that
g_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
.

Therefore, the class

\Omega^{(j)}:=\left\{g_{\varepsilon}\right\}_{\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}^{m}}

is composed of densities, and we have
\Omega^{(j)}\subset\mathcal{A}_{K}^{\beta}
.

For
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{2^{dj}}
, let
\omega(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
denote the Hamming distance

\displaystyle\omega(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{J}-1\}^{d}}\mathbbm{1}_{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}\neq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k}}}.

We consider the test function

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{J}-1\}^{d}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}2^{-j(d/2+1)}\frac{\Psi_{\mathbf{k}}}{\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}}.

By construction, we have
\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\leq 1
, and therefore
f_{\varepsilon}
is
1
-Lipschitz.
Consequently, the Wasserstein distance between
g_{\varepsilon}
and
g_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
is lower-bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{1}(g_{\varepsilon},g_{\varepsilon^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\varepsilon}(u)(g_{\varepsilon}(u)-g_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(u))\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{J}-1\}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}2^{-j(d/2+1)}(\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}})^{-1}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}\psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u)c_{1}(\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k}})\psi_{\mathbf{k}}(u)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=2^{-j(d/2+1)}(\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}})^{-1}c_{1}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\{0,\ldots,2^{J}-1\}^{d}}2\mathbbm{1}_{\varepsilon_{\mathbf{k}}\neq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathbf{k}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}(u)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=2c_{K}2^{-j(\beta+d+1)}(\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}})^{-1}\omega(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}).

Using the Varshamov-Gilbert Lemma
(
Tsybakov 2008
, Lemma 2.9, p. 104)
provides the existence of a subset
\Omega^{(j)}_{\ast}\subset\Omega^{(j)}
such that

•

M_{\ast}:=|\Omega^{(j)}_{\ast}|\geq 2^{m/8}
;

•

for all distinct
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Omega^{(j)}_{\ast}
,
\omega(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})\geq m/8
.

Next, according to
(
Uppal et al. 2019
, Lemma 24 - Supplementary Material)
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(g_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes n}\mid g_{0}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle=n\mathrm{KL}(g_{\varepsilon}\mid g_{0})

\displaystyle\leq cn\|g_{0}-g_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}/2

\displaystyle\leq ncc_{1}^{2}2^{dj}

\displaystyle\leq nc_{K}2^{-2j\beta},

where
g_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes n}
denotes the
n
-times tensor product of
g_{\varepsilon}
with itself.
Choosing
j:=\lfloor\log_{2}(n)/(2\beta+d)\rfloor
thus yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(g_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes n}\mid g_{0}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle\leq c_{K}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{10}\log M_{\ast},

provided
c_{K}
is small enough.
A direct application of Fano-Birgé Lemma
(
Tsybakov 2008
, Theorem 2.5)
hence entails

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{p}_{n}}\sup_{\varepsilon\in\Omega^{(j)}_{\ast}}\mathbb{E}_{g_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes n}}\mathrm{W}_{1}(g_{\varepsilon},\widehat{p}_{n})

\displaystyle\geq(1/4)(2^{dj}/8)(c_{K}2^{-j(\beta+d+1)}/\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}})

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime}_{K}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}},

which yields the announced result.
∎
